<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>9. CREDIT FACILITY</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On November&nbsp;16, 2007, Archipelago Learning, LLC (formerly Study Island, LLC), a subsidiary of
the Company (the &#147;Borrower&#148;), entered into an $80.0&nbsp;million credit facility with General Electric
Capital Corporation, as agent, composed of a $70.0&nbsp;million term loan and a $10.0&nbsp;million revolving
credit facility, which expires in November&nbsp;2013. The proceeds of the term loan and $4.9&nbsp;million in
cash were used to pay a distribution of $73.2&nbsp;million to the Class&nbsp;A shareholders of the LLC and
debt financing costs of $1.7&nbsp;million. The term loan bears interest at rates based upon either a
base rate or LIBOR plus an applicable margin (3.75% as of December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 3.25% as of
December&nbsp;31, 2009 and December&nbsp;31, 2008, in each case for a LIBOR-based term loan) determined
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->82<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#D80413tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">
<!-- xbrl -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">based
on the Borrower&#146;s leverage ratio. Amounts under the revolving credit facility can be borrowed and
repaid, from time to time, at the Borrower&#146;s option, subject to the pro forma compliance with
certain financial covenants.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In May&nbsp;2009, the credit agreement (the &#147;Credit Agreement&#148;) governing the term loan and the
revolving credit facility was amended to permit the creation of AL Midco, LLC, (&#147;AL Midco&#148;), a new
wholly-owned subsidiary of the LLC, which assumed all of the LLC&#146;s interests in the Borrower. AL
Midco became a guarantor under the Credit Agreement and the LLC was released as a guarantor. In
November&nbsp;2009, the Credit Agreement was further amended to permit the sale of TeacherWeb. This
amendment further modified certain terms of the Credit Agreement, including adding a LIBOR floor of
1.25% to the calculation of the interest rates and reducing the letter of credit sublimit available
to the Borrower under the Credit Agreement from $2.0&nbsp;million to $1.0&nbsp;million. In addition, the
Borrower repaid term loans in an aggregate amount of $6.5&nbsp;million upon the consummation of the sale
of TeacherWeb, which was completed in November&nbsp;2009. As a result of the sale, TeacherWeb was
released as a guarantor.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In June&nbsp;2010, the Credit Agreement was further amended to permit the acquisition of
EducationCity and to add a $15.0&nbsp;million supplemental term loan and an additional $10.0&nbsp;million to
the revolving credit facility, both of which were drawn in order to finance the acquisition. The
Borrower subsequently repaid the $10.0&nbsp;million revolving credit facility during the quarter ended
September&nbsp;30, 2010. This amendment further modified certain terms of the Credit Agreement,
including increasing the applicable margin on all loans by 0.50%, modifying the definition of
permitted acquisitions, and increasing the letter of credit sublimit available to the Borrower
under the Credit Agreement from $1.0&nbsp;million to $2.0&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Credit Agreement is secured on a first-priority basis by security interests (subject to
permitted liens) in substantially all tangible and intangible assets owned by the Borrower and AL
Midco. In addition, any future domestic subsidiaries of the Borrower will be required (subject to
certain exceptions) to guarantee the Credit Agreement and grant liens on substantially all of its
assets to secure such guarantee.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Credit Agreement requires the Borrower to maintain certain financial ratios, including a
leverage ratio (based on the ratio of consolidated indebtedness, net of cash and cash equivalents
subject to control agreements, to consolidated EBITDA, defined in the Credit Agreement as
consolidated net income adjusted by adding back interest expense, taxes, depreciation expenses,
amortization expenses and certain other non-recurring or otherwise permitted fees and charges), an
interest coverage ratio (based on the ratio of consolidated EBITDA to consolidated interest
expense, as defined in the Credit Agreement) and a fixed charge coverage ratio (based on the ratio
of consolidated EBITDA to consolidated fixed charges, as defined in the Credit Agreement).
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Credit Agreement contains certain affirmative and negative covenants applicable to AL
Midco, the Borrower and the Borrower&#146;s subsidiaries that, among other things, limit the incurrence
of additional indebtedness, liens on property, sale and leaseback transactions, investments, loans
and advances, merger or consolidation, asset sales, acquisitions, dividends, transactions with
affiliates, prepayments of subordinated indebtedness, modifications of the Borrower&#146;s
organizational documents and restrictions on the Borrower&#146;s subsidiaries. The Credit Agreement
contains events of default that are customary for similar credit facilities, including a
cross-default provision with respect to any other indebtedness and an event of default that would
be triggered by a change of control, as defined in the Credit Agreement. As of December&nbsp;31, 2010,
the Borrower was in compliance with all covenants. The Credit Agreement is secured on a
first-priority basis by security interests (subject to permitted liens) in substantially all of the
assets of the Borrower and AL Midco.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Borrower has the right to optionally prepay its borrowings under the Credit Agreement,
subject to the procedures set forth in the Credit Agreement. The Borrower may be required to make
prepayments on its borrowings under the Credit Agreement if it receives proceeds as a result of
certain asset sales, additional debt issuances, or events of loss. In addition, a mandatory
prepayment of borrowings under the Credit Agreement is required each fiscal year in an amount equal
to (i)&nbsp;75% of excess cash flow (as defined by the Credit Agreement) if the leverage ratio as of the
last day of the fiscal year is greater than 4.00 to 1.00, (ii)&nbsp;50% of excess cash flow if the
leverage ratio as of the last day of the fiscal year is less than or equal to 4.00 to 1.00 but
greater than 3.25 to 1.00, or (iii)&nbsp;25% of excess cash flow if the leverage ratio as of the last
day of the fiscal year is less than or equal to 3.25 to 1.00. No mandatory prepayment is required
if the leverage ratio is less than or equal to 2.50 to 1.00 on the last day of the fiscal year. The
Borrower will not be required to make a mandatory prepayment related to the years ended
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->83<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>

31, 2010 and 2009. The Borrower made a mandatory prepayment of $0.5&nbsp;million during the second
quarter of 2009 related to the excess cash flow generated for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>10. TAXES</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Prior to the Reorganization, the LLC was not a taxpaying entity for federal income tax
purposes. As a result, the LLC&#146;s income was taxed to its members in their individual federal income
tax returns. No federal income tax expense was recognized in 2008. Upon the Reorganization, the
Company became a taxpaying entity and a net deferred tax liability of $2.0&nbsp;million was recognized
into tax expense in the consolidated statement of income for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2009. The
Company&#146;s discontinued operation, TeacherWeb, was a taxpaying entity for federal income tax
purposes.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company has not recorded a valuation allowance against its deferred tax assets, as it
believes that it is more likely than not that all deferred tax assets will be realized in future
periods. A review of all positive and negative evidence of realizability was considered in
determining the need for a valuation allowance. Furthermore, the weight given to the potential
effect of such evidence was commensurate with the extent to which it can be objectively verified.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
FASB issued an amendment to FASB ASC 740, Income Taxes,
 which clarifies the accounting and disclosure for uncertainty in tax positions, as
defined. The Company adopted the provisions of FASB ASC 740, as amended, as of January&nbsp;1, 2007, and
has analyzed filing positions in all of the federal and state jurisdictions where the Company is
required to file income tax returns, as well as all open tax years in these jurisdictions. Upon
adoption, the Company identified no uncertain tax positions and therefore recorded no cumulative
effect adjustment related to the adoption of this amendment. The Company&#146;s recognizes interest
accrued related to unrecognized tax benefits and penalties as income tax expense.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2010, the Company partially recognized its previously recorded
unrecognized tax benefit based on new information related to recent court rulings which support the
Company&#146;s position. The Company is not currently under examination in any federal or state income
tax jurisdiction. It is reasonably possible
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">that the ending balance of unrecognized tax benefits will decrease in the following 12&nbsp;month
period, following the resolution to an inquiry with the state taxing authority. A
reconciliation of the beginning and ending amounts of unrecognized tax benefits is as follows, for
the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 2009 (in thousands):

state jurisdictions. Federal income tax returns for 2006 through 2009 remain open to examination,
and state, local, and U.K. income tax returns for 2005 through 2009 remain open to examination.

<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>12. STOCKHOLDERS&#146; EQUITY</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company&#146;s capital structure consists of preferred stock and common stock, which includes
unrestricted and restricted shares. No shares of preferred stock have been issued as of December
31, 2010. The restricted common stock carries full voting rights and participation rights to
dividends, but may not be sold until vested.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The LLC&#146;s capital structure consisted of Class&nbsp;A Shares, Class&nbsp;A-2 Shares, Class&nbsp;B Shares and
Class&nbsp;C Shares. The terms of the shares were governed by the LLC Agreement. On January&nbsp;10, 2007, 109,545,064 Class&nbsp;A Shares were
issued to the Company&#146;s private-equity sponsor and other investors. Class&nbsp;A-2 Shares were issued in
connection with the acquisition of TeacherWeb. Class&nbsp;B Shares and Class&nbsp;C Shares were held by
employees of the LLC. Upon the Reorganization, each of the
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->86<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#D80413tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>
<!-- xbrl -->

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">members of the LLC contributed their respective classes of shares in the LLC in exchange for
common stock and restricted common stock in the Company, with the amount of shares determined based
on the shares available in the Company and the liquidation value of the LLC, as defined by the LLC
Agreement. The Class&nbsp;A and Class&nbsp;A-2 shareholders received shares of common stock. The Class&nbsp;B
shareholders received common stock for the shares for which they had met the performance vesting
requirements and restricted common stock, with the same performance vesting schedule, for their
remaining shares. The non-executive Class&nbsp;C shareholders received common stock for their shares.
The executive Class&nbsp;C shareholders received restricted common stock subject to vesting based on,
among other things, the cash returns to a stockholder on their stock. The exchange resulted in no
additional compensation expense, as the fair value of the common stock received did not exceed the
fair value of the Class&nbsp;B and Class&nbsp;C shares exchanged on the date of the Reorganization.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Contributions and Distributions</I></B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On October&nbsp;16, 2009, the LLC made a special distribution of $8.0&nbsp;million to its equity holders
representing a return on such holders&#146; investment in the LLC, which was paid in accordance with the
terms of the LLC Agreement. In addition, a total of $3.8&nbsp;million in distributions were made to the
members of the LLC in order to meet their tax obligations for the period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As part of the Reorganization, a subsidiary of a Class&nbsp;A shareholder (the &#147;Merger Entity&#148;) was
merged into the Company and dissolved. The Merger Entity had $0.7&nbsp;million of cash and a tax payable
of $0.5&nbsp;million. The Merger Entity&#146;s net assets of
$0.2&nbsp;million were treated as a contribution on
the Company&#146;s consolidated statement of equity for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2009.
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>13. EARNINGS PER SHARE</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Subsequent to the Reorganization, earnings per share is computed using the two-class method,
considering the restricted common shares, due to their participation rights in dividends of the
Company. Under this method, the Company&#146;s net income is reduced by the portion of net income
attributable to the restricted common shares, and this amount is divided by the weighted average
shares of common stock outstanding. Prior to the Reorganization, there were various classes of
stock, which were entitled to earnings based on a priority established in the LLC&#146;s LLC agreement.
The Class&nbsp;A shares were entitled to a return of capital and a preferred return before any other
class of shares was entitled to earnings. The Class&nbsp;A shares were entitled to 100% of the earnings
for the period from January&nbsp;1, 2007 to the Reorganization. Earnings per share was calculated based
on the shares of common stock that were exchanged for the Class&nbsp;A shares in the Reorganization for
all periods prior to the Reorganization.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The components of earnings per share are as follows for the years ended December&nbsp;31 (in
thousands, except per share data):

    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
</TR>
<!-- End Table Body -->
</TABLE>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">For the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 2009, options for 655,406 and 68,128 weighted-average
shares were excluded from the diluted earnings per share calculation, respectively, as their effect
was antidilutive. No options were outstanding during the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008.
</DIV>


<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>14. STOCK-BASED COMPENSATION</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>2007 Equity Compensation Plan</I></B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2007, the Board of Managers of the LLC adopted the 2007 Equity Compensation Plan
(the &#147;Plan&#148;). Under the terms of the Plan, eligible participants, as determined by the Board,
received grants of Class&nbsp;B Shares and Class&nbsp;C Shares, which together are defined as the
Participation Shares. The purpose of the Plan was to compensate employees and consultants of the
LLC. Under the terms and provisions of the LLC Agreement, the Participation Shares were to be
considered profits interests in the LLC and each holder of a share is considered a member of the
LLC.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->88<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#D80413tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>
<!-- xbrl -->

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For the awards granted under the Plan, each Class&nbsp;B Share vested 20% on each anniversary as
specified in the Participation Stock Agreement. The Class&nbsp;C shares were subject to performance
hurdles and holders of Class&nbsp;C shares were entitled to distributions after holders of Class&nbsp;A and
Class&nbsp;A-2 shares received certain threshold multiples of cash-based returns on their respective
Class&nbsp;A and Class&nbsp;A-2 shares, subject to such Class&nbsp;C share holders&#146; continued employment by or
service to the Company. For each Class&nbsp;B Share granted to a participant, the participant also
received one Class&nbsp;C Share. All Class&nbsp;C shares and any unvested Class&nbsp;B shares were forfeited if
any participant was no longer an employee of the Company. All Class&nbsp;B and Class&nbsp;C shares were
forfeited if the participant&#146;s employment was terminated by the Company for cause or by the
participant without good reason.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>2009 Omnibus Incentive Plan</I></B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On November&nbsp;17, 2009, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors and shareholders approved the 2009
Omnibus Incentive Plan (the &#147;Incentive Plan&#148;). The Incentive Plan authorizes the Board to grant
common stock, restricted common stock and options to purchase common stock to employees, directors
or consultants of the Company, as compensation for services to be rendered. Under the Incentive
Plan, 2,198,172 shares of common stock are authorized to be issued and 1,530,823 shares were
available for additional issuance at December&nbsp;31, 2010. Upon exercise of options, new shares are
issued to employees.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Employee Stock Purchase Plan</I></B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On November&nbsp;17, 2009, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors and shareholders approved the Employee
Stock Purchase Plan (&#147;ESPP&#148;). Under the ESPP, employees could elect to withhold amounts from their
compensation to purchase shares of the Company at the end of six-month periods, at 100% of the fair
market value of the stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On June&nbsp;9, 2010, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors and shareholders approved the Amended and
Restated ESPP. Under the Amended and Restated ESPP, the purchase price for each share is be equal
to 85% of the lesser of the fair market value of a share on the first day of the offering period
and the fair market value of a share on the last day of the offering period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Under the ESPP and Amended and Restated ESPP, 500,000 shares of common stock are authorized to
be purchased. As of December&nbsp;31, 2010, 497,379 shares were available for additional issuance.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;<B><I>Stock-Based Compensation Expense</I></B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company recognizes compensation expense for the grant-date fair value of the awards over
the service period of the awards, which is generally the vesting period. The fair values of stock
options are calculated using the Black-Scholes option pricing model (&#147;Black-Scholes&#148;). The Company
did not apply a forfeiture rate to its restricted common shares or its stock options as a
significant portion of these awards were granted to a few key executives.
</DIV>

over a weighted average period of 2.97&nbsp;years.

<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>15. EMPLOYEE BENEFIT PLANS</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company provides a 401(k) defined contribution retirement plan for all eligible employees
who meet certain eligibility requirements, including performing three months of service. The
Company matches 100% up to 3% of employee contributions, plus 50% of the amount of the
participant&#146;s deferred compensation that exceeds 3% of the participant&#146;s compensation, but not in
excess of 5% of the participant&#146;s compensation.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Participants are 100% vested in the portion of the plan representing employee and employer
safe harbor match contributions. For the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2010, 2009 and 2008, the Company
made contributions of $0.5&nbsp;million, $0.4&nbsp;million and $0.2&nbsp;million, respectively, under this plan.
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>16. BUSINESS SEGMENT DATA AND GEOGRAPHICAL INFORMATION</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Pursuant to the acquisition of EducationCity on June&nbsp;9, 2010, the Company operates as three
operating segments, Study Island, Educationcity Limited (a U.K. private company) and Educationcity
Inc. (an Illinois company). The Company aggregated the three operating segments to one reportable
segment based on the similar nature of the products, content and technical production processes,
types of customers, methods used to distribute the products, and similar rates of profitability.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The three operating segments offer subscription-based online products that provide
instruction, practice, assessment and productivity tools for teachers and students. The content and
engineering teams operate in a similar manner to enhance and maintain the products. The primary
customer bases for all three operating segments are schools. The markets for the United States and
United Kingdom are both English-speaking, which is important from a product marketing and
development perspective. The operating segments have similar rates of profitability.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Geographical areas are North America (which includes operations of the United States and
Canada) and the United Kingdom. The following geographical area information includes revenues based
on the physical location of the operations (in thousands):
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Considering the Company&#146;s global nature, and its exposure to foreign currencies, the financial
results in the United Kingdom can be impacted by changes in currency exchange rates.
</DIV>


<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>17. RELATED-PARTY TRANSACTIONS</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company is a party to an agreement with a stockholder to provide advisory services in
connection with the identification, evaluation and acquisition of one or more businesses. The
stockholder receives a transaction fee upon the successful consummation of any transaction that
they identify. The amount of this transaction fee is dependent upon the size of the acquisition.
Additionally, any reasonable expenses incurred in connection with this agreement are reimbursed.
During the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2010, the Company incurred and paid an aggregate of $1.5&nbsp;million
under this agreement related to the acquisition of EducationCity. During the years ended December
31, 2009 and 2008, the Company did not incur or make any payments under this agreement.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Providence Equity Partners paid for certain costs related to travel and other expenses of
members of the Company&#146;s Board of Directors and other staff assisting those Directors in certain
oversight functions and invoiced the Company for reimbursement. During the year ended December&nbsp;31,
2010, $0.2&nbsp;million of these costs were paid by the Company. No payments were made during the year
ended December&nbsp;31, 2009 or 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company purchases equipment from an affiliate of Providence Equity Partners. Equipment
purchases with this supplier totaled $1.3&nbsp;million, $0.7&nbsp;million and $0.3&nbsp;million for the years
ended December&nbsp;31, 2010, 2009 and 2008, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;During the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2010, the Company made certain tax payments of $0.2&nbsp;million
to states on behalf of shareholders of the LLC for periods prior to the Reorganization. These
amounts were invoiced to these stockholders for reimbursement.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As part of the sale of TeacherWeb, the Company signed a transition services agreement with
Edline whereby the Company performed certain accounting and administrative functions related to
TeacherWeb for a period not to exceed six months from the sale. In May&nbsp;2010, the transition
services agreement was extended to July&nbsp;1, 2010. The transition services agreement was subsequently
amended in August and September&nbsp;2010, extending its term through October&nbsp;31, 2010. During the
transition period, certain costs were paid by the Company on behalf of TeacherWeb, which were
billed to and reimbursed by Edline. The Company received no fee for the performance of these
services.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 2009, the Company paid $1.0&nbsp;million and $0.2
million, respectively, to TeacherWeb vendors on behalf of Edline, of which a total of $0.1&nbsp;million
was receivable from Edline as of December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 2009, and is recorded in other current
assets on the consolidated balance sheet.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;EducationCity U.K. leases office space in Rutland, U.K. which is owned by the pension funds of
two officers and stockholders of the Company. The Company made payments under this lease for $0.1
million for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2010. The Company concluded during purchase accounting
that this lease is a market based lease.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In connection with the purchase of EducationCity, the Company incurred a $5.0&nbsp;million note
payable to the sellers, payable in equal installments on December&nbsp;31, 2010 and 2011. Upon the
purchase, the sellers became officers of the Company and stockholders. As of December&nbsp;31, 2010,
the remaining balance to be paid under that note payable was $2.5&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,n -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>18. SUBSEQUENT EVENTS</B>
</DIV>

<!-- xbrl,body -->
<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On January&nbsp;6, 2011, the Company announced that Mr.&nbsp;Mark S. Dubrow, would be named Chief
Financial Officer and Executive Vice President of the Company, effective on January&nbsp;31, 2011.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Also on January&nbsp;6, 2011, the Company announced that Mr.&nbsp;James B. Walburg would be retiring
from his position as the Company&#146;s Chief Financial Officer, effective on January&nbsp;31, 2011. Mr.
Walburg will continue on in a consulting role for three months following his retirement (the
&#147;Transition Period&#148;). In connection with his retirement, Mr.&nbsp;Walburg entered into a separation
agreement with the Company, dated as of January&nbsp;4, 2011 (the &#147;Separation Agreement&#148;), pursuant to
which Mr.&nbsp;Walburg would be entitled to his base salary and benefits consistent with the terms of
his existing employment agreement during the Transition Period. Under the terms of the
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->92<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#D80413tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>
<!-- xbrl -->

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Separation Agreement, following the Transition Period or if Mr.&nbsp;Walburg is terminated during
the Transition Period without cause, Mr.&nbsp;Walburg shall be entitled to continued payment of his base
salary for a period of 12&nbsp;months, payment of any bonus to which he was entitled for the 2010 fiscal
year, and the vesting of 50% of his restricted common stock subject to time-based vesting on
January&nbsp;10, 2011, the vesting of 50% of his restricted common stock subject to time-based vesting
on January&nbsp;10, 2012, and the vesting of all of his restricted common stock subject to vesting based
on performance measures on January&nbsp;10, 2011.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On January&nbsp;10, 2011, the Company granted stock options for 434,976 shares of common stock to
its officers and employees as a part of their compensation for 2010, at an exercise price of $10.09
per share. The options vest over a four year term. On January&nbsp;31, 2011, the Company granted stock
options for 125,000 shares of common stock to Mark Dubrow, Chief Financial Officer and Executive
Vice President of the Company, at an exercise price of $10.38 per share. The options vest over a
four year term.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On February&nbsp;24, 2011, the Company granted an aggregate of 28,038 restricted stock units to Mr.
Timothy McEwen, Chief Executive Officer of the Company, as part his annual compensation for 2010.
Subject to Mr.&nbsp;McEwen&#146;s continued employment, 41 2/3% of the restricted stock units will be settled
in cash six months after the grant date on August&nbsp;24, 2011, and the remaining restricted stock
units will be settled in common stock of the Company four years from the grant date on February&nbsp;24,
2015. Pursuant to the terms of the restricted stock unit agreement, Mr.&nbsp;McEwen will receive 28,038
dividend equivalent rights. Each dividend equivalent right relates to one restricted stock unit and
entitles him to an amount equal to the per share dividend, if any, paid by the Company during the
period between the grant date and vesting date of the related restricted stock unit. Each dividend
equivalent right will vest and be payable in cash at the time that the related restricted stock
unit is settled.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On March&nbsp;3, 2011, Matthew Drakard resigned as Managing Director and Co-Founder of
Educationcity Limited. Mr.&nbsp;Drakard&#146;s resignation from EducationCity in any capacity will be
effective as of December&nbsp;31, 2011. Until then, he will continue as a part-time employee in an
advisory capacity. On March&nbsp;4, 2011, Simon Booley resigned as Managing Director and Co-Founder of
EducationCity, Ltd. Mr.&nbsp;Booley will continue to work for EducationCity as a part-time employee.
Effective on March&nbsp;1, 2011, Richard Whalley will serve as the U.K. Managing Director for
EducationCity.
</DIV>
